# Walkthrough

## 2026-09-08 21:09:20 +08:00 — NPC parity audit

- Inspected the current NeoForge working tree without incorporating the user's pre-existing uncommitted implementation changes into this commit.
- Fetched and pinned CitizensDev/Citizens2 upstream at `e2998fd5fb8486b2551c1bcd366e37c263a6b35f`; compared all main-module command declarations, including commands in versioned traits, and source counterparts.
- Read this project's Claude Code session history, memory, and port plan; cross-checked historical completion claims against the actual old plugin data and current code.
- Read the old Arclight plugin snapshot and deployed server data. Confirmed continuity of all 200 old NPC identities, deployed jar content equivalence, and partial companion-service coverage. Production files were not modified.
- Added `tools/audit_npc_parity.py`, opt-in dialogue acceptance probes, a Gradle init script for those probes, and `docs/npc-parity-audit-2026-09-08.md` with command/class/trait evidence tables.
- Reasoning: inventory and successful compilation do not establish execution semantics. Trace command consumers, transaction outcomes, saved-progress readers, configuration consumers, and actual service entry points. Treat stale comments/session summaries as hypotheses rather than proof.
- Verification: forced 112-test baseline passed. Four dialogue acceptance probes executed with three reproducible failures (two sound IDs and cooldown preservation). Two isolated test-server runs produced 103/106 and 105/106 passing assertions; knockback fixture failures remain unresolved. Isolated command lookup confirmed Bukkit-style `minecraft:give` and `minecraft:setblock` roots absent while their native counterparts exist.
- Result: audit acceptance fails. Implementation defects are documented, not represented as fixed. No production deployment or permission-system rewrite was performed. See the report for the full list of open gates and evidence limits.

## 2026-09-08 21:12:41 +08:00 — Preserve the existing implementation baseline

## 2026-09-09 10:25:19 +08:00 — Preserve legacy dialogue state and playback semantics

## 2026-09-09 10:27:06 +08:00 — Resolve legacy vanilla command roots

- Normalize a leading slash and Bukkit minecraft: command roots only when the namespaced root is absent and the native root exists. Apply this before dispatch and after alias rewriting; preserve all arguments, item/component namespaces, and registered mod commands.
- Fixed alias-result tokenization to recognize all whitespace, including tabs.
- Validation: ten dialogue regression tests pass and build succeeds. This repairs root lookup only; legacy NBT arguments, nested command migration, external command services and action failure propagation remain separate work.
## 2026-09-09 10:37:54 +08:00 — Preflight dialogue actions and propagate execution failures

- Prepare all actions before executing any. Reserve combined item costs against copied inventory, reject malformed amounts, and preflight delayed line actions before immediate payments. Missing saved items, unavailable economy/aliases, unknown commands, and syntax errors prevent payment from starting.
- Stop remaining actions and end the session on execution failure. Observe Minecraft command callbacks from server ticks; defer option actions out of the selecting command's execution queue. Failed sessions do not record completion.
- Honor explicitly named online saved-item/economy recipients. Added balance inquiries, exact configured currency scaling and economy exception propagation; unavailable currencies no longer invent a default identifier. Invocation keys are unique, with no automatic retry after uncertain outcomes.
- Added opt-in runtime probes using NeoForge FakePlayer, real inventories and the actual Minecraft dispatcher. Verified successful namespaced give and purchase, missing reward, insufficient items, runtime command failure, invalid command arguments, unavailable economy, delayed reward preflight, and deferred option execution.
- Validation: 15 dialogue regression tests pass; 10/10 dedicated-server action probes pass; 112 ordinary tests and the final build pass. Confirmed the release Interactions jar excludes ActionRuntimeAudit. Existing general NPC fixture remains 103/106, with three knockback failures.
- Limits: this is dependency preflight and failure propagation, not an atomic transaction spanning arbitrary commands, inventory and external services. Earlier successful side effects and separate lines are not rolled back. Offline recipients are explicitly rejected; successful economy integration still needs the real companion mod test. The audit report tracks remaining feature gates. No production deployment or live-server modification.
## 2026-09-09 20:43:27 +08:00 — Restore guard regeneration and make knockback acceptance reliable

- Verified Sentinel 2.9.0-b522 bytecode: regeneration adds one hit point after the elapsed timer exceeds healRate, only below maximum health; non-positive rates disable healing. Restored this persisted option with native tick timing and scaled-health support.
- Apply configured maximum/current guard health immediately at spawn, including when ScaledMaxHealthTrait was just attached. Apply invincible in both directions so ordinary guards do not retain Citizens' default protection.
- Diagnosed the knockback fixture without changing NPC motion logic. Entity sections became queryable later than ENTITY_TICKING chunks; the old damage/check schedules could run before either pig was visible. Once ready, the disabled actor stayed still and the control moved about 1.78 blocks. The original selector box still intersected that control, producing a false failure.
- Versioned the repaired fixture overlay: wait for both actors with a bounded timeout, assert damage success, compare centre-coordinate displacement, and retain existence assertions. Declared Gradle overlay inputs/outputs so edits actually reach the test world. Added required marker checks that fail the task on missing/failed runtime assertions.
- Verification: all 107 NPC scenario assertions, 10 dialogue action probes, and 10 new Sentinel probes pass on both NeoForge 21.1.233 and 21.1.248. On 21.1.248, all 15 dialogue regression tests and 112 ordinary tests pass; build succeeds. Verified that release jars exclude runtime probes.
- Production server/data were not modified. Full modpack/client behavior, remaining Sentinel features, permission/service bridges, and the other audit gates remain open. Read-only Paradigm inspection confirmed its versioned public API exposes permission queries but no primary-group mutation method; an additive alias would not reproduce GroupManager semantics.

## 2026-09-10 09:50:09 +08:00 — Abort rejected native shop payments and compensate completed transactions

- MoneyAction now reports rejected deposits and withdrawals as execution failures. Successful compensation restores both the player wallet and the finite shop balance; rejected compensation leaves accounting unchanged and is logged for reconciliation.
- Composed costs recheck each part immediately before execution, abort subsequent actions on failure, and undo completed actions in reverse order. Refund failures do not prevent other refunds, and preserve the original execution exception. Completed compensation is cleared to prevent duplicate refunds.
- Native shop purchases catch action failures, undo completed rewards and costs, and do not count failed purchases. NPC command charges stop on execution failure; already queued commands also stop after an earlier charge failure.
- Added five transaction regression tests and eight isolated dedicated-server shop probes using a controllable economy provider and synthetic reward action through the actual shop click path. They cover debit/credit accounting, both compensation directions, rejected payment, rejected refund, unavailable reward, and successful purchase.
- Validation on NeoForge 21.1.248: 117 ordinary tests pass; build succeeds; 107 NPC assertions and all 36 runtime probes pass. Release jars contain no runtime audit classes. Logs: artifacts/repair-shop-runtime248.log and artifacts/repair-shop-final248.log.
- Limits: external providers must accurately report whether mutations succeeded. Compensation is best effort, not a durable cross-service atomic transaction; failed refunds require reconciliation. The real Yuuniverse economy provider bridge, delayed command acceptance coverage, and remaining parity gates are still open. Production remains unchanged.

## 2026-09-10 09:55:07 +08:00 — Connect native Citizens transactions to the optional Yuuniverse economy API

- Added an optional reflection adapter for native shop and command MoneyAction operations. It uses the published EconomyApi interface and configured default currency ID, symbol, and minor-unit scale; it requires neither the dialogue mod nor an economy compile dependency.
- Install at ServerStarted, after the economy publishes its service during ServerStarting. Preserve an existing custom provider and only unregister the adapter instance owned by Citizens at shutdown.
- Convert amounts exactly using BigDecimal; reject negative, non-finite, overflowing, or excess-precision mutations. Propagate rejected ledger operations as false so the transaction repairs abort rewards. Give each invocation a unique operation key and never retry uncertain writes. Balance lookup failure is unavailable (-1), not a fabricated balance.
- Verified public method signatures against the production Economy 0.4.1 jar without modifying production. Added three regression tests covering configured precision, reflected credit/debit/balance calls, unique keys, formatting, rejected writes, zero-value operations, and unavailable balance lookup.
- Validation on NeoForge 21.1.248: 120 ordinary tests pass and final build succeeds. The isolated server without the optional economy mod passes all 107 NPC assertions and 36 runtime probes. Release jars exclude audit probes.
- Remaining acceptance: run with the real economy mod and isolated ledger to verify service lifecycle, frozen/missing accounts, successful payments, refunds, and persistence. This change does not establish complete integration or full Citizens/plugin parity. Production remains unchanged.

## 2026-09-10 09:59:05 +08:00 — Preserve decimal NPC prices in batch purchases and refunds

- Replaced binary floating-point multiplication/division in MoneyAction with decimal arithmetic. Prices such as 0.1 repeated three times now produce 0.3, and a balance of 0.3 can afford three purchases at 0.1.
- Apply decimal addition/subtraction to finite shop balances so compensation restores the original decimal value. Validate that the resulting amount is representable by the double-based EconomyProvider before calling the external mutation; do not round large unrepresentable balances or mutate the wallet before detecting accounting overflow.
- Reject negative or non-finite persisted prices and negative repeat counts; reject non-finite editor input. Keep zero-cost actions valid and unavailable economy balances unaffordable.
- Added four regression tests for repeated decimal prices, affordability boundaries, compensation arithmetic, numeric range failures and invalid action configuration.
- Validation on NeoForge 21.1.248: 124 tests pass, final build succeeds, and the isolated server passes all 107 NPC assertions and 36 runtime probes. Release jars contain no runtime probes. Logs: artifacts/repair-money-final248.log and artifacts/repair-money-arithmetic-runtime248.log.
- Real-economy ledger integration acceptance and the other full parity gates remain open. Production was not modified.

## 2026-09-10 16:42:34 +08:00 — Restore configured dialogue radius and entry checks

- Inspected original Interactions 2.14.1 bytecode. Proximity entry scans run every 20 ticks; positive start radius enables entry, zero disables it. The entry marker is set before attempting a conversation, avoiding repeats while the player remains in the same conversation region. End radius zero disables distance termination; nonzero boundaries have no extra half-block allowance.
- Added proximity scanning over spawned Citizens NPCs, matching conversations by ID or name, selecting the nearest eligible NPC in the same level, and clearing entry state on region exit/logout/reload/stop. Click and proximity entry share existing cooldown checks plus legacy permission and air-start checks. The permission node is interactions.start.<conversation id>; the old plugin tests solid ground one block below the player's position when air starts are disabled.
- Corrected the startRadius unit comment, removed its incorrect claim that all migrated files use zero, and fixed session termination at zero/exact end radius.
- Verification on NeoForge 21.1.248: 16 dialogue tests, 124 ordinary tests and normal build pass. All 107 NPC assertions and 40 runtime probes pass, including four new actual-session tests for exact boundary, just outside boundary, unlimited distance and removed NPC. Release artifacts exclude runtime probes.
- Acceptance still needed: automatic entry/re-entry with real connected players, permission/air checks across modded blocks, movement locking, global dialogue restrictions and other original parity gates. No production changes. The report explicitly separates these remaining flows from the verified session-distance behavior.

## 2026-09-11 00:38:45 +08:00 — Enforce dialogue horizontal movement restrictions in the packet handler

- Implemented block_movement for unmounted players with a session-owned restriction map and a separate Interactions mixin. The injection runs on the server thread after vanilla validates movement values and handles pending teleport acknowledgements. Horizontal displacement is rejected with the normal position confirmation; look-only and vertical-only packets continue through vanilla processing.

## 2026-09-11 12:22:01 +08:00 — Restore dialogue routing and manual-duration semantics

- Found an active migration defect in legacy story files such as Prelina apartment day one: line-level start_conversation was ignored. The legacy scheduler completes the terminal line's last_actions before an automatic jump; start_options instead borrows another node's choices and takes precedence over a jump. Added these fields to the parser and implemented their separate routing paths without playing the borrowed node's dialogue.
- Corrected conditional_dialogue from replacement text to ordered conditional redirects. Legacy bytecode checks nonempty requirements before showing the original line or executing its actions; the first matching redirect wins. Missing targets stop before the source actions. Conditional and automatic cycles advance through server ticks instead of recursive calls.
- Added terminal-route validation before line actions, retained last-action failure propagation, and reset routing state when changing nodes. Earlier sequential lines do not apply their terminal-route fields, matching the inspected legacy ordering.
- Corrected time: -1 to wait for manual advancement instead of completing after one tick. Other timed lines keep the existing scheduler behavior.
- Validation: 25 dialogue audit tests passed; added 18 routing runtime checks plus a manual-wait check. Final isolated acceptance on NeoForge 21.1.248 passed all 107 NPC assertions and 108 runtime probes. Evidence: artifacts/repair-routing-runtime248-final.log, artifacts/repair-routing-tests248.log and artifacts/repair-routing-dialogue-tests.xml.
- The first runtime run failed the pre-existing pathfind-minecraft-detour assertion (106/107) while its initial routing checks passed. The final rerun passed that unchanged pathfinding check and the expanded routing suite. The earlier failure remains recorded and its cause is not established; it is not counted as successful acceptance.
- Actual connected-client story playthroughs, complete reward/service migration, inventory restrictions, option presentation and full modpack coverage remain open. No production data was modified.
- Final validation: 124 ordinary tests and normal build passed; release jars contain no runtime audit classes. Evidence: artifacts/repair-routing-final248.log.

## 2026-09-11 12:25:05 +08:00 — Validate option destinations before executing actions

- Found that a selected option ran its actions before resolving start_conversation. A misspelled destination therefore consumed payment or issued rewards and then recorded a successful completion because missing targets and intentional end options shared the same null result.
- Resolve an explicitly configured option destination before any selected-option actions execute. A missing destination logs the configuration error and ends unsuccessfully. An omitted destination retains its intentional end behavior; valid destinations still execute actions and enter the next node.
- Added three real-inventory/progress runtime assertions: missing targets preserve both payment and rewards, missing targets do not mark the source completed, and valid targets still consume payment, grant the reward and enter the target node.
- All 107 NPC assertions and 111 runtime probes passed on NeoForge 21.1.248, including the previously intermittent pathfind-minecraft-detour check. Evidence: artifacts/repair-option-target-runtime248.log. This does not establish why that earlier failure occurred.
- Remaining scope includes broader dialogue UI/global restrictions, reward/service migration and real-client full-modpack acceptance. No production files changed.
- Final validation: 124 ordinary tests and normal build pass; release jars exclude runtime audit classes. Evidence: artifacts/repair-option-target-final248.log.

### 2026-09-12 17:46:36 +08:00 — Issue the first vanilla path request with its required ground state

Diagnostics show PathB remained at the start with navigation already stopped, and only one world entity carried that name, linked to the expected NPC. This rules out a duplicate/orphan actor explaining that observed failure.
- MCNavigationStrategy deferred its path request until update(), but setOnGround(true) ran earlier in its constructor. Physics could clear that flag before the request, causing GroundPathNavigation to refuse the path. Moved the existing ground-state initialization to immediately before the first actual request; it is not forced on subsequent navigation ticks. This preserves the upstream navigation precondition at the time it is needed.
- Added a direct regression probe that clears the ground flag after strategy construction, verifies vanilla refuses the same request, then verifies the deferred strategy request creates an active path without cancellation. Retained the original arena detour assertion and added fixture-relative path/identity diagnostics. Final results follow below.

The new navigation probe reproduces vanilla rejection after a ground reset and confirms successful deferred initiation; PathB then moves from its start, rounds the wall and passes the unchanged arrival assertion. Hanging survival/control checks and all JSON display/payment probes pass. Earlier failed runs remain retained as diagnostic evidence.
- Final dialogue audit: all 37 tests pass, including legacy show_text hover value arrays (artifacts/repair-json-dialogue248-final.log and artifacts/repair-json-dialogue-tests.xml).

- Continued the Citizens work from 7797d8b. Restored `/waypoints hpa` and `/wp hpa` using the seven CitizensAPI hierarchical graph types and native block sources. Full block coordinates and native dimension bounds replace the old height assumptions. Every valid boundary crossing is retained, including steps crossing horizontal and vertical boundaries together; higher edges stay inside their parent footprint. Queries reuse portals, settle the goal before accepting it, recursively refine valid block paths and remove only temporary memberships, including after interruption. Dirty-chunk rebuilding covers every overlapping region even with an unaligned origin. Failed refinement returns no path.

One server-owned worker performs graph construction/search. Configurable region, request and timeout limits default to 4, 4 and 30 seconds. Completion, cancellation and shutdown validate exact request, NPC, registry, player-session, world and permission ownership. Permission/custom-provider exceptions retire the request and report failure without escaping into the server tick. Command feedback uses translation resources.

## 2026-10-03 13:11:33 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Preserve NPC definitions during allocation and failed reloads

- Continued the active Citizens goal from 86ac135 after the completed HPA batch. Reviewed the current requirements against native storage and lifecycle code. Found that automatic allocation ignored unloaded source records, a malformed UUID aborted later restoration, native creation veto cleanup could erase its own source, and global reload removed live NPCs before learning that its file could not be read. Added negative coverage; the old implementation failed 11 of 17 focused tests. The initial runner's preparation check also used a stale LASTEXITCODE after a PowerShell-only script; preparation had succeeded and the native run was then started normally.
- Added a shared nonnegative ID allocator for memory and persistent stores. It accounts for explicitly registered NPCs and every stored numeric ID, including unavailable or malformed definitions, and handles Integer.MAX_VALUE by finding a free ID without overflow. Invalid hints cannot override reserved IDs. The memory-store boundary expectation was clarified to continue its previous allocation cursor rather than require the lowest free value; the final behavior is documented and tested. Explicit-ID API overwrite semantics remain available.
- Made restoration isolate invalid UUIDs, missing names/trait lists, unavailable types, duplicate UUIDs, ambiguous numeric keys, creation vetoes and recoverable load exceptions. Each NPC loads from a detached record, with the accepted legacy entity type normalized before trait loading. Failed native creation/load cleanup cannot delete its source; partial save publication and recursive source reload are rejected while restoration owns the record. Unique numeric key spellings survive saves/removal. Explicit removal after successful restoration still clears data. No replacement NPC or invented provider data is used for an unavailable definition.
- YAML loading now validates and fully copies a document before replacing live data. Non-map roots, duplicate/string-colliding keys and recursive collection aliases are rejected; shared acyclic aliases and intentionally empty files remain valid. Failed NPC source reads or invalid NPC-section shapes restore the previous snapshot and throw. Citizens performs that source read before removing NPCs, abandoning editors or clearing pending respawns; command failure is observable, and a corrected source can be retried successfully. Custom stores must honor the documented failure contract.
- Final native provider absence/presence/absence runs pass 64/66/64 checks on NeoForge 21.1.248 and 63/66/64 on default 21.1.233, 387 checks total. The first run includes a source-initialization assertion. The synthetic returning provider is an actual registered Cow-based entity with native attributes: its NPC keeps its UUID, spawns as that entity when present and preserves its complete definition when removed again. The fixture also proves native creation veto recovery, identity collisions, loose legacy types, source immutability, and failed/successful reloads of normal and packet NPCs through real command/tick paths.
- Sequential regressions pass 305 creation and 62 removal checks; general acceptance passes 107 NPC assertions and 223 probes. All 261 ordinary tests and 100 Interactions tests pass with no failures/errors/skips. The default-version clean test/build succeeds, and all 14 frozen production/fixture inputs match their tested hashes. The consolidated verifier initially rejected the existing creation suite's two intentional listener exceptions; source inspection confirmed their explicit fixtures. It now requires those exact creation/removal messages for that suite and rejects all other event errors. Original negative logs and native diagnostics remain retained.
- Interactions stays in its own source set, mod ID and NeoForge JAR with one-way dependence on Citizens. No dialogue implementation changed. Citizens archives contain no Interactions classes/mixin resource, and Interactions contains no Citizens classes. Interactions archive entry names and contents match the preceding release. Configuration/shop failure handling, individual trait/provider contracts and registry lifecycles also retain targeted work, alongside the wider live service/data/client/modpack/proxy acceptance. ## 2026-10-03 13:56:51 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Repair default NPC store replacement and runtime ownership

- Continued the active Citizens goal from 92c784e. Rechecked the user's Interactions boundary against source sets, mod descriptors and built archives: dialogue remains under neoforge/src/dialogue with its own interactions mod ID and yuuniverse-interactions JAR. The dependency is Interactions → Citizens. This batch changes Citizens persistence and references only; it does not move dialogue features into the NPC mod.
- Reproduced the default-store defect in an isolated native server: the old setter changed the store field without replacing/loading the registry. Added startup overrides and provisional registry loading. Successful replacement saves the old registry and publishes a matching new store/registry; rejected loading or saving retains the previous binding and live NPC objects. Early and late startup overrides load once after levels are ready and do not overwrite unloaded source data. Null, reentrant and off-thread operations are rejected; assigning the current store is a no-op.
- Added permanent runtime retirement that clears both registry indexes before callbacks, prevents stale creation/saving/respawning, releases entities, traits, listeners and temporary lifetimes, and preserves persisted definitions. A vetoed despawn cannot retain an orphan entity. Failures after publication propagate with the new binding active. Named, anonymous and temporary registries keep their own lifetimes; parent traits release their own helpers. Respawn queues now distinguish exact NPC instances when registries share UUIDs. Tick iteration snapshots and checks ownership so a switch from an NPC update cannot continue ticking retired siblings.
- Source review found that loaded click redirects could capture an old-registry NPC or lose a forward/missing target. A dedicated native case reproduced the stale reference. Persisted redirects now retain their UUID and resolve through their owning registry when used; missing targets survive saving and recover when available, and player-filter links follow the resolved parent. Constructor-supplied targets retain their explicit-reference behavior.
- A real filesystem test reproduced synchronous YAML write failure being printed but swallowed. Such I/O failures now propagate through SimpleNPCDataStore.saveToDiskImmediate, allowing a store switch to reject an unsuccessful save. The test verifies that the memory snapshot and obstructing directory remain intact. Backend writes and external editor/provider callbacks are not rolled back; this batch does not implement atomic filesystem writes or strict serialization of every trait.
- Final native checks pass 78 runtime, 81 early-startup and 81 late-startup assertions on each of NeoForge 21.1.248 and default 21.1.233, 480 checks total. The fixture covers observable custom stores, normal/packet entities, canceled creation, partial candidate failure, saved references, shared-UUID queues, cleanup failures and real tick callbacks. The original binding and redirect failures, the failing filesystem test, intermediate successful matrices and a corrected PowerShell argument-array setup are retained as diagnostic evidence. The setup error stopped Gradle configuration before a server ran.

The verifier requires exactly the creation suite's two intentional listener errors and rejects unexpected update/event errors. Every Gradle/server invocation exited before the next; no validation process remains pending.

## 2026-10-04 20:23:31 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Publish YAML snapshots atomically and in order

- Continued Citizens persistence work from 1fccc20. A failing YAML getter previously truncated the destination before serialization finished, and independently scheduled writes could overwrite newer snapshots or race a reload. Negative tests reproduced truncation and missing save/reload barriers. The first binary-snapshot negative assertion failed prematurely because the fixture's synthetic bean tag was rejected; after restricting a tag inspector to that fixture class, a second negative run reproduced mutable binary aliasing and missing ordering across storage instances. The fixture error is not production defect evidence.
- YamlStorage now writes UTF-8 YAML/header content to a temporary sibling, flushes and forces the channel, copies supported existing permissions/ACLs and hidden/system flags, then atomically replaces the destination. Failed publication retains the previous file and cleans temporary files. Read-only/directory targets are rejected, existing symlinks resolve to their target and dangling links are preserved on failure. There is no destructive fallback for unsupported atomic replacement, and new files use the same publication path.
- Pending writes share an order by resolved file path across storage instances. A failed request does not poison retries; reload waits for preceding writes. Synchronous serialization stays on its caller's thread after waiting, while asynchronous serialization uses the common pool and reports failure. ## 2026-10-04 20:54:05 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Reduce repeated NPC batch snapshot copies

- Continued from f361484 after measuring the remaining batch-save cost. The opt-in native fixture creates 64/128/256/512 unspawned pig NPCs with nested synthetic unavailable-provider payloads and global data. Two warmups and three samples per operation measure elapsed time and current-thread heap allocation. Before changes, the 512-NPC in-memory batch median was 1401.07 ms and 2920.40 MB: every native save copied the full enclosing document twice inside an already isolated store batch, which also reparsed all record IDs for each NPC.
- Added DataKey.writeAtomically with a full-copy default for other implementations. MemoryDataKey takes a complete outer snapshot, then stages nested writers by accessed branches. It prepares all detached changes before applying them to the unpublished parent. Inner failure preserves parent changes and supports retry; outer failure discards completed inner writes. Final publication detaches the whole document, and copyWithRoot retains its full-copy contract. Raw map/list/array edits, map views, whole-root replacement, literal dotted keys, global namespaces and retained writer references preserve isolation.
- Native NPC saves and SimpleNPCDataStore now use the writer scope. Existing registration checks, strict serialization, removal intent and recursive-save guards remain. A per-batch record index reuses parsed IDs only while the entire key-name set matches, rebuilding after additions/deletions/renames, including same-count renaming. Tests verify preserved numeric spelling/unknown data and rejection of newly ambiguous keys before the next writer runs. Key-set comparisons still scan names, so this is not a claim of fully linear registry work.

## 2026-10-05 03:04:47 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Preserve runtime NPCs across provider reload failures

Ordinary reload previously removed live NPCs before asking the provider to restore its records. Reproduced this against deb5021: the new isolated fixture fails failed_reload_preserves_identity. Reload now stages a candidate registry, publishes only after loading succeeds, and retires old or failed runtime ownership without deleting stored definitions. Scoped queue cleanup preserves unrelated registries' pending respawns. Errors during candidate cleanup are suppressed on the original failure; cleanup failures after publication report the published state instead of reverting to retired objects. Integrations must reacquire the default registry after successful reload. Configuration and skin invalidation remain before loading; full configuration/shop transactions and shutdown cleanup remain unfinished.

## 2026-10-05 03:32:30 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Complete shutdown cleanup despite save and callback failures

Citizens now isolates shutdown operations, logs each failure, and continues cleanup without aborting the shared NeoForge stopping event. It captures all registries before callbacks, retires default/named/anonymous/temporary runtime ownership despite despawn vetoes, clears bindings/selections/respawn queues, and releases listeners, scheduler, API and providers. Capturing the registry snapshot before saving prevents a provider callback from removing a named registry to evade retirement. Shutdown rejects registry/data reentry and duplicate stopping notifications; next startup resets save-on-disable state. Automatic persistence remains owned by the default store.

Editor and prompt shutdown now continues after callback failures, aggregates errors, closes session admission, and unregisters a retained dispatcher. Ordinary reload remains available, and shutdown callbacks cannot reopen either service. Scheduler shutdown cancels retained sync/async handles, rejects new work and safely supports shutdown inside a tick callback. Async work still must cooperate with interruption. LuckPerms bridge cleanup releases owned references while preserving replacement providers. Anonymous NPC selections are explicitly cleared.

Settings now rejects failed storage reads, stages every live value until defaults/migrations save successfully, and retains duration caches/messaging state on read/write failure. Explicit saving is blocked after a failed load until successful recovery. Reload reads shop storage before replacing NPC runtime ownership; failed shop reads preserve current NPCs/maps and block stale overwrites. Shop serialization publishes atomically through DataKey.writeAtomically.

Investigation found ShopTrait.onRemove deleted the live per-NPC shop when failed candidates or old registries were retired. Added Trait.onRetire, defaulting to legacy onRemove(DESTROYED) cleanup; ShopTrait overrides it to retain independent persisted records. Actual NPC destruction and explicit trait removal retain shop-deletion behavior. This is an additive lifecycle contract for traits that own independent records, rather than suppressing generic cleanup callbacks.

## 2026-10-05 06:19:36 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Bind shop views and transactions to private stock ownership

Shop sessions now track both the definition and the exact NPCShopStorage. Extracted ShopSessionOwner for weak targets, transaction counts, suspension and retirement. Retired storage rejects routed trades, transactions and new inventory viewers while keeping persisted values readable. Removal aggregates private cleanup and definition-deletion failures.

Default-store replacement preflights affected stock transactions before invoking the provider. Suspension tokens cannot resume a later suspension or a retired owner.

## 2026-10-05 09:00:14 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Cancel queued commands when shop actions roll back

Inspection corrected an earlier assumption: CommandAction already used Util.runCommand to schedule next-tick dispatch. The prior removal-preflight audit called destructive APIs/dispatchers directly during a transaction and did not exercise this built-in queued path. Successful real command actions already supported deferred NPC removal. The actual missing behavior was cancellation: commands from failed costs/results still ran after the purchase had rolled back.

Added Util.scheduleCommand returning a cancellation handle while preserving the void runCommand API. CommandAction tracks handles per transaction, cancels them on rollback, and cancels partial batches if scheduling/refresh throws. Reusing one action across purchases does not mix their handles. Command lists are snapshotted, order/repeats and execution permissions are preserved, and debug output now describes scheduling accurately. Console commands now expand player-sensitive variables from the supplied clicker while retaining the console execution source; player-source interpolation remains single-pass.

Validation: both NeoForge 21.1.233 and 21.1.248 pass 26 native checks with real CommandAction instances. Coverage includes failed costs/results, throwing later actions, partial-batch failure, same-action isolation, source permissions, legacy API behavior, console clicker placeholders and actual one-shot chest/merchant purchases. Failed destructive purchases refund items and retain the NPC; successful ones remove the NPC after payment updates and return merchant remainders once. The original CommandAction fails rolled_back_commands_never_dispatch in the negative control. Removal-preflight regression passes 55 checks. All 320 ordinary and 100 Interactions tests pass with zero failures/errors/skips; clean test/build succeeds. All audit/test processes terminated; the unrelated server was untouched.

All 78 Interactions archive entry names/contents are unchanged, preserving its independent NeoForge JAR and one-way Interactions -> Citizens dependency. ZIP container hashes differ after rebuild. Original Citizens save hash is unchanged. Details: docs/shop-command-rollback-2026-10-05.md; reproduction: tools/shop-command-runtime-audit/README.md; local evidence: artifacts/shop-command-validation-summary.json and artifacts/verify-shop-command.ps1. No subagents were used.

This is cancellation before dispatch, not undo of commands already executed, a durable queue/retry system, or rollback of arbitrary post-commit/provider side effects. Physical-client/provider acceptance and full reload transactionality remain separate work. The full Citizens goal remains active.

## 2026-10-05 11:04:18 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Finish shop settlement before notifying listeners

Purchases now record limits, release transaction ownership and finish native settlement before success messages/events. Receipt and event failures are logged independently rather than erasing purchase bookkeeping or escaping into merchant payment handling. Post-commit listeners can remove the owning NPC legitimately; pre-commit destructive actions remain guarded. Merchant costs update a bounded view of real input slots, excluding the result preview, with no stale copy-back after callbacks close or replace the screen.

If a merchant closes while later actions/refunds remain, transaction-local escrow holds newly produced items. Failed rewards can still be reclaimed before final release; successful settlement is visible before purchase notification. Remaining items follow vanilla inventory return/drop behavior, including full inventories. OpenShopAction refreshes returned inventory after closure and cancels its queued opening on rollback, while preserving its existing immediate origin closure. It does not reconstruct that closed origin on failure.

Load recognizes the storage layer's contiguous numeric-map representation of lists, including empty lists, while preserving non-list maps.

## 2026-10-06 16:52:10 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Bind automatic commands to their actual NPC instance

Template and scheduled commands previously inserted an ID which RequirementsProcessor resolved from the default registry. A target from a named, anonymous or temporary registry could therefore modify a different NPC with the same ID. Shared preparation now identifies automatic unqualified targets. Dispatch carries the exact NPC instance through a short-lived scope consumed at the native command bridge, then stores it in the command context. Permission and ownership checks still run normally. Explicit IDs, UUIDs, positional selectors and registry qualifiers retain their normal lookup behavior. Dispatch and requirements validate registry identity; a delayed command for a removed instance cannot affect a replacement even if both IDs match. Nested explicit commands clear the outer binding, and scopes restore on success/failure without leaking into later callbacks.

The expanded native fixture also exposed an existing extra-space parsing issue: whitespace before a subcommand was treated as the modifier. CommandManager now skips only leading empty argument tokens before selecting the modifier; quoted arguments retain their existing parser semantics. Native input matching occurs before that parsing, preserving the exact dispatch binding.

Validation: ordinary test/build passed with 538 tests, zero failures/errors/skips (`artifacts/command-instance-build.log`). Both NeoForge 21.1.233 and 21.1.248 passed 342 isolated creation checks (`artifacts/command-instance-native-233.log`, `artifacts/command-instance-native-248.log`). Interactions acceptance passed 100 tests (`artifacts/command-instance-interactions.log`). ## 2026-10-06 16:58:39 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Keep placeholder evaluation on the bound command target

## 2026-10-06 17:15:08 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Retire template event subscriptions on server shutdown

Applied template event actions previously left their consumers registered on NeoForge's global bus, including actions from superseded template registries. CommandEventAction now owns the exact registered consumer and unregister operation, supports terminal close, clears target UUIDs before unregistering and tracks all active registrations independently of the current registry. Shutdown closes admission before collecting the active set, attempts every cleanup and aggregates errors. Failed unregistration stays disabled and can be retried. Closed objects reject reapplication. Citizens invokes this shutdown before other teardown work and starts a new subscription lifetime on server startup; only new actions can register in that lifetime.

Validation: both NeoForge 21.1.233 and 21.1.248 pass 63 checks in the real isolated shutdown fixture (`artifacts/template-events-shutdown-native-233.log`, `artifacts/template-events-shutdown-native-248.log`). New native cases verify active/superseded subscriptions before stop, no callbacks after stop, and rejection of retained/new subscriptions during shutdown. Unit tests cover exact consumer unregistration, idempotent close, failure isolation/retry and reopening admission without reviving old actions. Ordinary test/build passes 544 tests with zero failures/errors/skips (`artifacts/template-event-shutdown-build.log`); Interactions acceptance passes 100 tests (`artifacts/template-events-shutdown-interactions.log`). Final normal binary/source artifacts contain no audit probes. Citizens contains 855 Citizens classes and zero Interactions classes; the independent Interactions JAR contains 70 Interactions classes and zero Citizens classes.

The fixture README, lifecycle documentation and remaining-work checklist now describe this ownership boundary. Template reload subscription migration, full integrated-server restart and physical-client/provider acceptance remain open; this change preserves current reload behavior and does not claim those are complete.

## 2026-10-06 17:34:27 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Scope template event subscriptions to registry identity

Template event actions previously filtered only by UUID, allowing an unrelated NPC in another registry with the same UUID to run the action. Subscriptions now use weak registry identity keys containing UUID sets, avoiding both equality-based registry conflation and strong retention of discarded registries. Existing same-registry UUID continuity is preserved.

Default-registry reload and default-store replacement now prepare a subscription transfer before candidate loading. Both old and candidate bindings are available during preparation; rejected candidates lose their subscriptions before retirement. Publication merges subscriptions added to the old registry during loading, removes old registry bindings and retains unrelated registries. Cleanup errors after publication do not discard the committed subscription transfer. This is registry ownership migration, not replacement of applied template command definitions.

Validation: both NeoForge 21.1.233 and 21.1.248 pass the expanded 57-check reload fixture (`artifacts/template-event-registry-native-233.log`, `artifacts/template-event-registry-native-248.log`). ## 2026-10-06 17:54:20 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Replace event subscriptions when reapplying a template

A newly loaded Template object with an existing key previously added its event commands alongside the earlier object's subscriptions. Template now defers event installation until its other actions finish, then replaces bindings for that key, registry and UUID before final respawn. Other NPCs and other template keys retain their commands. Definitions without events remove previous bindings, repeated application is idempotent, and last-binding removal unregisters replaced listeners. Ordinary action failure preserves the previous subscriptions; registration failure rolls back bindings added during the attempt and supports retry. Cleanup errors after publication are reported without undoing installed bindings.

An interaction with registry transfer was also addressed: explicit candidate reapplication (including removing all events) is recorded during the active transfer, so commit cannot merge obsolete subscriptions back into that candidate. Late subscriptions for other targets still merge normally.

Validation: both NeoForge 21.1.233 and 21.1.248 pass 345 creation/runtime checks (`artifacts/template-reapplication-native-233.log`, `artifacts/template-reapplication-native-248.log`). Four unit tests cover scoped replacement/idempotency, ordinary action failure, registration failure/retry, and candidate publication respecting replacement/deletion. Ordinary test/build passes 550 tests with zero failures/errors/skips (`artifacts/template-reapplication-build.log`); Interactions acceptance passes 100 tests (`artifacts/template-reapplication-interactions.log`). Release binary/source JARs contain no runtime audit probes. Citizens has 857 Citizens classes and zero Interactions classes; the independent Interactions JAR has 70 Interactions classes and zero Citizens classes.

The lifecycle documentation and remaining-work checklist distinguish explicit reapplication from file reload alone. ## 2026-10-06 18:00:17 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Guard template reentry across reloaded instances

## 2026-10-06 18:17:40 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Report tpto success only after completed transport

The native tpto command unconditionally sent success after requesting an NPC/entity teleport, even when cancellation prevented movement. NPC transport now uses the existing completion-aware API and verifies current NPC/entity and sender identity before feedback. Native player/entity transport uses EntityUtil's confirmed result. ## 2026-10-07 03:37:36 +08:00 (UTC+08:00) — Handle cooldown duration values and first use correctly

Cooldown behavior previously reparsed numeric expression results as unitless decimal strings, so ordinary Double millisecond results could fail. It also treated an absent record as epoch zero, blocking first use for sufficiently long durations.
